        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: VibeComfy Template Porting Workbench

## Overview
VibeComfy already has the raw pieces of a template-porting workflow: `convert`, `validate`, `doctor`, schema-backed validation, model asset extraction, node pack install planning, generated ready-template rendering in `tools/format_as_python.py`, and WanAnimate-specific regression tests. The first useful slice should not be a separate app or a raw JSON runner. It should promote these scattered checks into a first-class CLI/tooling path that imports a Comfy workflow, emits a Python VibeWorkflow scratchpad/ready-template, preserves provenance, and reports the failures that currently cost RunPod time: missing node packs, bad/missing model URLs, unresolved helper nodes, positional widget alias drift, and missing required schema inputs.

The simplest approach is to build a package-level `vibecomfy.porting` workbench and a `vibecomfy port ...` CLI command that reuses existing code rather than inventing a second converter. `tools/format_as_python.py` is the current best emitter, but because it lives under `tools/`, the reusable core should move into package modules while the old tool remains as a thin compatibility driver.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Shared Port Report and Python Emission

### Step 1: Add a porting report model (`vibecomfy/porting/report.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Define small dataclasses for `PortReport`, `PortIssue`, `PortArtifact`, and `AssetCheckResult` with stable JSON serialization.
2. Include fields for source path/id, detected workflow shape, source hash, normalized API node count, emitted Python path, custom-node pack suggestions, unresolved class types, model assets, schema issues, helper-node rewrites, and widget alias observations.
3. Keep issue severities aligned with existing validation semantics: `error`, `warning`, `info`.

### Step 2: Move reusable emitter logic into package code (`vibecomfy/porting/emitter.py`, `tools/format_as_python.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extract the reusable portions of `tools/format_as_python.py` into package code, preserving deterministic output, explicit node IDs, `_node(...)` helper behavior, `_extras` for non-identifier inputs, and `READY_METADATA` / `READY_REQUIREMENTS` emission.
2. Keep `tools/format_as_python.py` and `tools/convert_ready_templates.py` as wrappers around the package API so existing workflows still work.
3. Reuse `tools/_widget_schema.py` by moving it to `vibecomfy/porting/widget_schema.py` or exposing a package import path, then update old tool imports.

### Step 3: Add a workbench orchestrator (`vibecomfy/porting/workbench.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Implement `analyze_source(...)` to load a workflow via `load_workflow_json`, `normalize_to_api`, and `convert_to_vibe_format`, using `get_schema_provider("auto")` when available.
2. Implement `emit_python_scratchpad(...)` that writes a Python-esque VibeWorkflow scratchpad, not a wrapper that re-loads the original JSON at runtime.
3. Preserve provenance in `READY_METADATA`: source path/id, source hash, original shape, conversion timestamp or converter version, source workflow id when known, and whether schema was available.
4. Populate `READY_REQUIREMENTS` from inferred requirements plus extracted structured model assets.

## Phase 2: Diagnostics — Catch Current Failure Classes Before RunPod

### Step 4: Custom node pack inference (`vibecomfy/porting/workbench.py`, `vibecomfy/node_packs_install.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Reuse `missing_class_types_for_workflow(...)` and `missing_packs_for_workflow(...)` to report installable packs and unresolved class types.
2. Include pack repo, pip packages, and matched classes in the port report.
3. Surface unresolved classes as hard errors for `port check` and warnings for `port convert --allow-unresolved`.

### Step 5: Model asset inference and HEAD checks (`vibecomfy/model_assets.py`, `vibecomfy/porting/assets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Reuse `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` and `entries_from_scratchpad_path(...)` to collect structured model assets.
2. Add an opt-in URL checker using `httpx.Client().head(..., follow_redirects=True)` with deterministic timeout and no download body.
3. Report status codes, redirected final URL, missing URL fields, duplicate asset targets, and license-gated/404 cases as actionable report entries.
4. Keep HEAD checks disabled in normal unit tests and local conversion unless `--head-check-models` is passed.

### Step 6: Helper-node resolution diagnostics (`vibecomfy/workflow.py`, `vibecomfy/porting/workbench.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep existing compile-time stripping/rewriting for `Note`, `MarkdownNote`, `SetNode`, and `GetNode`.
2. Add a report pass that counts removed UI-only nodes and lists unresolved `GetNode` / `SetNode` references whose broadcast source cannot be resolved.
3. Treat dangling helper rewrites as `error` because they create links to absent runtime nodes.
4. Add a narrow helper classification extension point for future reroute/broadcast nodes without changing runtime compile behavior for unknown classes.

### Step 7: Positional widget alias detection (`vibecomfy/porting/widget_aliases.py`, `vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py`, `vibecomfy/workflow.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Centralize positional widget alias logic so `normalize_to_api`, `VibeWorkflow.compile`, and the Python emitter use the same mapping source.
2. Prefer schema-derived input order from `object_info` when available; fall back to the committed static widget schema for offline operation.
3. Add a diagnostic pass that compiles the workflow, compares emitted input names against schema inputs, and reports unresolved `widget_N` inputs where a required named input is missing.
4. Move the existing Wan/WanAnimate aliases out of the private `_POSITIONAL_WIDGET_ALIASES` map in `workflow.py` into the shared module, preserving current behavior.

### Step 8: Schema preflight integration (`vibecomfy/schema/validate.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/validate.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Reuse existing `missing_required_input`, `unknown_input`, `unknown_class_type`, `invalid_link_shape`, and type mismatch checks in the port report.
2. Add optional `--port-report` or shared formatter support so `validate` / `doctor` can show the same actionable categories without duplicating logic.
3. Keep `validate` focused and cheap; make the fuller workbench report the new `port check` path.

## Phase 3: CLI and Live Validation Loop

### Step 9: Add explicit CLI entry points (`vibecomfy/commands/port.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Register a new explicit command module in `COMMANDS`.
2. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port check <workflow> [--json] [--head-check-models]` to analyze without writing.
3. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port convert <workflow> --out out/scratchpads/name.py [--json] [--head-check-models]` to emit Python and include the report.
4. Add `--ready-id kind/name` for users intentionally creating a ready-template-shaped module under `ready_templates/`.
5. Return nonzero for hard errors: unresolved class types, unresolved helper broadcasts, schema-required missing inputs, failed Python build, or failed emitted scratchpad validation.

### Step 10: Wire into the run/RunPod preflight (`vibecomfy/commands/run.py`, `scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Before queueing a workflow, keep existing `workflow.validate(schema_provider=...)` as the hard gate.
2. Add a cheap port-report summary to `doctor` and RunPod matrix logs so future failures show whether they are model URL, custom-node, schema, or helper-node problems.
3. Do not make network HEAD checks mandatory for `run`; keep them opt-in via `port check --head-check-models` and in targeted preflight jobs.
4. In RunPod corpus matrix logs, copy the emitted scratchpad and port report next to the existing convert/validate logs.

## Phase 4: Battle Tests and Documentation

### Step 11: Add focused unit and CLI tests (`tests/test_porting_workbench.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_workflow_core.py`, `tests/test_schema.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test a simple fixture based on `image/z_image` or a tiny synthetic workflow: check report shape, provenance, Python emission, import/build, compile, and validate.
2. Test the current WanAnimate production-parity template id `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_wan_animate_preprocess_kijai`: helper nodes must not survive compiled API, no dangling links to removed nodes, node packs include WanAnimate/SAM/KJ/VHS requirements, and model assets include detection assets.
3. Test positional widget alias diagnostics with a fake schema provider so no Comfy runtime is required.
4. Test model HEAD checks with `httpx.MockTransport` or injected client behavior, not real network.
5. Add CLI smoke tests for `port check --json` and `port convert --out ... --json`.

### Step 12: Document the first slice and roadmap (`docs/template_porting_workbench.md`, `docs/adding_templates_models.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document the new import → convert → check → validate loop.
2. Show the recommended commands for a source workflow, a generated scratchpad, a ready-template candidate, and the WanAnimate battle test.
3. Record the remaining roadmap: richer source discovery, broader helper-node catalog, schema snapshots per node-pack version, automatic PR-ready manifest edits, and optional CI job for URL HEAD checks.

## Execution Order
1. Create the report model and package emitter first so the CLI can be thin.
2. Add workbench analysis before adding writes, because `port check` is the cheapest validation path.
3. Add `port convert` after emitted Python can import/build/validate in tests.
4. Add optional network HEAD checks only after offline report behavior is stable.
5. Wire `doctor` / RunPod logs after the report shape is settled.
6. Finish with docs and the WanAnimate regression path.

## Validation Order
1. Run focused tests first: `pytest -q tests/test_porting_workbench.py tests/test_workflow_core.py tests/test_schema.py`.
2. Run CLI tests: `pytest -q tests/test_cli.py`.
3. Run ready-template regressions: `pytest -q tests/test_ready_templates.py`.
4. Run node/model checks: `pytest -q tests/test_nodes_install.py tests/test_model_assets.py tests/test_models_registry.py`.
5. Run the full local suite with `pytest` once the command and report wiring are in place.
6. Optional/manual: `python -m vibecomfy.cli port check video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_wan_animate_preprocess_kijai --json` and, when network is acceptable, repeat with `--head-check-models`.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-143",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-156",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-164",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: route support states: the revised step 5 says to classify ltx control rows as vibecomfy_supported, wgp_only, vibecomfy_unsupported, new, or blocked, but the current worker routesupportstate enum at reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:17-20 only supports wgp_only, vibecomfy_supported, and vibecomfy_unsupported. the plan needs to either extend the enum/report schema for new/blocked or keep new/blocked strictly as fixture disposition values mapped to an existing runtime support state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-9-control-rail-and-20260506-1802"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-165",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: startup tests: step 11 still needs sharper wording for `vibecomfy_memory_profile`. the current startup template exports it only inside `if [ \"$reigh_backend\" = \"vibecomfy\" ] && [[ \"$reigh_worker_profile\" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]` at worker_startup.template.sh lines 28-30, while the revised plan says to assert it is exported consistently when proving both wgp and vibecomfy render from the same template. if implemented as an unconditional wgp+vibecomfy export assertion, the test would conflict with the current template's intentional conditional behavior.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-12-dual-executor-20260507-0226"

    }

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-153",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: worker integration: the migration doc describes the per-call profile override gap as involving reigh-worker adapter handling of override_profile before constructing vibecomfy sessionconfig, but the plan limits sprint 1 per-run work to the vibecomfy cli/runtime. that may be acceptable for a vibecomfy-library mvp, but it does not fully exercise the production-facing per-task override semantics called out in the overall context.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-155",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: a residual issue-hint tension remains for resolution handling. the plan acknowledges current `vibecomfy run` lacks a resolution flag, but allows the adapter to leave resolution and other template-specific values unwired for sprint 2. because `z_image_turbo` and `qwen_image_2512` are marked vibecomfy-supported direct routes, this may not preserve the existing worker task-parameter contract for tasks that provide a non-default `resolution`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-154",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-vibecomfy-memory-20260506-0147"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-162",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked vibecomfy profile wording against `vibecomfy/tests/test_ready_templates.py`. the revised plan says to add the production template to representative compile profile coverage 'alongside the existing dry-run id', but the dry-run id is not currently in `profile_smoke_template_ids`; only `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_5b_i2v` and `video/wan_t2v` are. this is a small wording mismatch rather than a functional blocker because adding either the production id alone or both ids would still satisfy sprint 4.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-4-wan-single-frame-and-20260506-0818"
      ]
    }

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked direct queue caller values in `db_task_to_generation_task()`: direct image tasks may carry `resolution`, `steps`/`num_inference_steps`, `seed`, and `override_profile`. the revised adapter plan covers seed, steps, and memory profile but not guaranteed resolution propagation, so it does not handle all relevant direct-route caller arguments for a supported vibecomfy direct route.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
      ]
    }
  ],

        Escalated debt subsystems:
        [
  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 9,
    "plan_count": 6,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-156",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the direct adapter still has a correctness risk around resolution parity. current vibecomfy ready templates hardcode image dimensions (`image/z_image` uses `emptysd3latentimage` 1024x1024 and `image/qwen_image_2512` uses 768x768), while `vibecomfy run` exposes no `--resolution` flag; the revised plan says a temporary scratchpad/input file may be generated only if supported, otherwise resolution is left out. that means a vibecomfy-supported direct route can silently ignore the worker's `resolution` param unless scratchpad resolution patching is made mandatory for supported direct routes or the support is explicitly limited to default-resolution smoke fixtures.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2-adapter-seam-and-20260506-0249"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-164",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: route support states: the revised step 5 says to classify ltx control rows as vibecomfy_supported, wgp_only, vibecomfy_unsupported, new, or blocked, but the current worker routesupportstate enum at reigh-worker/source/task_handlers/tasks/template_routing.py:17-20 only supports wgp_only, vibecomfy_supported, and vibecomfy_unsupported. the plan needs to either extend the enum/report schema for new/blocked or keep new/blocked strictly as fixture disposition values mapped to an existing runtime support state.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-9-control-rail-and-20260506-1802"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-165",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: startup tests: step 11 still needs sharper wording for `vibecomfy_memory_profile`. the current startup template exports it only inside `if [ \"$reigh_backend\" = \"vibecomfy\" ] && [[ \"$reigh_worker_profile\" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]` at worker_startup.template.sh lines 28-30, while the revised plan says to assert it is exported consistently when proving both wgp and vibecomfy render from the same template. if implemented as an unconditional wgp+vibecomfy export assertion, the test would conflict with the current template's intentional conditional behavior.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-12-dual-executor-20260507-0226"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

  {
    "subsystem": "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure",
    "total_occurrences": 6,

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-014",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-142",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"

      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-154",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: documentation/data gap: the sprint exit criteria require profiles 1 and 3 to have vram and wall-clock data, but the plan puts that only in metadata as an info-level criterion and does not schedule a concrete artifact location or command for recording the measurements. the implementation could satisfy all unit tests while still missing one of the explicit exit criteria.",

        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-158",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting infrastructure for resolution is still incomplete if direct vibecomfy routes are considered production-supported. vibecomfy has a `vibecomfy.patches.resolution` helper and the cli can load scratchpad files with `allow_scratchpad=true`, but the plan does not require using that path; it permits leaving resolution out for sprint 2.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked vibecomfy profile wording against `vibecomfy/tests/test_ready_templates.py`. the revised plan says to add the production template to representative compile profile coverage 'alongside the existing dry-run id', but the dry-run id is not currently in `profile_smoke_template_ids`; only `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_5b_i2v` and `video/wan_t2v` are. this is a small wording mismatch rather than a functional blocker because adding either the production id alone or both ids would still satisfy sprint 4.",

  {
    "subsystem": "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them",
    "total_occurrences": 3,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-109",
        "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
Build a holistic VibeComfy template porting workbench/toolset, based on the failures seen while porting production Reigh workflows to VibeComfy. The toolset should make it easy to find/import source Comfy workflows, convert them into proper Python ready-template/scratchpad format, preserve provenance, infer and validate custom node packs, infer and HEAD-check model asset URLs, resolve UI-only/helper nodes like Note/MarkdownNote/SetNode/GetNode and similar reroute/broadcast helpers, detect positional widget aliases by comparing compiled API prompts with Comfy object_info/schema, surface missing required inputs before a RunPod burn, emit actionable reports and tests, and support battle-testing against both a simple template and the current WanAnimate production-parity template. It should be pragmatic: build the first useful slice now, use it immediately on the current failures, and document the remaining roadmap. Avoid raw JSON execution as the steady-state path; workflows should land as Python-esque VibeComfy ready templates/scratchpads. Include a strategy for integrating this into the live validation loop so future users avoid the same URL/model/custom-node/schema failures.

User notes and answers:
- User added: discoverability is a first-class requirement. The VibeComfy porting toolset must make it obvious to humans and agents when to use it: command names/help, README/authoring docs, AGENTS.md or skill guidance, and failure/live-validation docs should all point to port check before manual template editing or expensive RunPod validation. The tool should document when to use check, convert, doctor/validate, model URL checks, custom-node pack discovery, and Python-template conversion.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: VibeComfy Template Porting Workbench

## Overview
The critique does not show that the idea is targeting the wrong subsystem. The root cause is still that VibeComfy has the needed pieces split across `convert`, `doctor`, schema validation, node-pack planning, model asset extraction, and ready-template generation. The plan should stay centered on a first-class `vibecomfy port ...` workflow, but the implementation order must change: helper/UI nodes must be diagnosed separately from custom-node pack inference, model asset extraction must cover API-shaped sources as well as UI workflows, widget alias migration must include the parity tools, and existing `convert` behavior must remain compatible.

The first useful slice is a package-level `vibecomfy.porting` workbench plus a discoverable CLI surface. It should make `port check` the obvious cheap preflight before manual Python-template editing or expensive RunPod validation, while `port convert` emits Python-esque VibeWorkflow scratchpads/ready-template candidates rather than relying on the original JSON as the steady-state artifact. Existing lightweight `convert` and ready-template materialization paths should be preserved unless explicitly routed through the new porting command.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Report Shape, Source Loading, and Helper Semantics

### Step 1: Add a porting report model (`vibecomfy/porting/report.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Define dataclasses for `PortReport`, `PortIssue`, `PortArtifact`, `NodePackSuggestion`, and `AssetCheckResult` with stable JSON serialization.
2. Include source id/path, detected workflow shape, source hash, normalized API node count, emitted Python path, helper-node diagnostics, custom-node suggestions, unresolved runtime class types, model assets, schema issues, widget alias observations, and recommended next commands.
3. Keep issue severities aligned with current validation concepts: `error`, `warning`, and `info`.

### Step 2: Centralize helper/UI-node classification (`vibecomfy/porting/helpers.py`, `vibecomfy/workflow.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Move the helper-node class set and helper-resolution diagnostics into a reusable module that knows about `Note`, `MarkdownNote`, `SetNode`, and `GetNode`.
2. Add a diagnostic pass that inspects the original IR before compile and reports unresolved `GetNode` / `SetNode` broadcasts instead of letting compile silently skip dangling helper references.
3. Expose a helper-stripped runtime class view for downstream diagnostics so custom-node pack inference does not treat `Note`, `MarkdownNote`, `SetNode`, or `GetNode` as missing installable node packs.
4. Keep `VibeWorkflow.compile("api")` behavior compatible: resolved helper broadcasts still rewrite to direct links; helper nodes still do not survive into API prompts.

### Step 3: Expand model asset extraction coverage (`vibecomfy/model_assets.py`, `vibecomfy/porting/assets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep existing UI-workflow extraction from node `properties.models`.
2. Add API-shaped extraction from node class types and input fields using the same subdir rules already used for structured requirements.
3. Merge assets from raw UI JSON, normalized API prompts, loaded scratchpad/ready-template metadata, and `READY_REQUIREMENTS` without duplicates.
4. Mark assets inferred from filenames without URLs as actionable warnings so API prompt sources are not falsely reported as having complete URL provenance.

## Phase 2: Python Emission and Compatibility Boundaries

### Step 4: Move reusable ready-template emitter logic without breaking current callers (`vibecomfy/porting/emitter.py`, `tools/format_as_python.py`, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Extract reusable `_node(...)`-style Python emission from `tools/format_as_python.py` into package code.
2. Preserve explicit node IDs, deterministic ordering, `_extras` for non-identifier inputs, `READY_METADATA`, `READY_REQUIREMENTS`, registered input handling, and current override behavior.
3. Keep `tools/format_as_python.py` and `tools/convert_ready_templates.py` as compatibility wrappers around the package emitter.
4. Do not change the established `vibecomfy convert` contract in `vibecomfy/commands/convert.py`; the new explicit Python materialization behavior belongs to `vibecomfy port convert` first.

### Step 5: Preserve both conversion contracts (`vibecomfy/scratchpad_loader.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/convert.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/port.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Leave existing lightweight scratchpad rendering available for compatibility.
2. Implement `port convert` as the path that emits explicit Python VibeWorkflow construction from a loaded source workflow.
3. Support ready-template-shaped output with `--ready-id kind/name`, but default examples and docs to `out/scratchpads/...` so promotion to `ready_templates/` remains intentional.
4. Validate emitted Python by importing it, calling `build()`, compiling API, and running schema validation when available.

## Phase 3: Diagnostics — Catch Current Failure Classes Before RunPod

### Step 6: Add the workbench orchestrator (`vibecomfy/porting/workbench.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Implement `analyze_source(...)` to load ready ids, scratchpads, raw JSON paths, and indexed workflow references through existing loader/normalizer paths.
2. Preserve provenance in `READY_METADATA`: source path/id, source hash, original shape, source workflow id when known, converter version, and whether schema was available.
3. Run helper diagnostics before custom-node inference.
4. Run custom-node pack inference against the helper-stripped runtime class view, while reporting unresolved helper broadcasts separately as helper errors.
5. Reuse `workflow.validate(schema_provider=...)` for schema issues and include formatted `missing_required_input`, `unknown_input`, `unknown_class_type`, `invalid_link_shape`, and type mismatch results in the report.

### Step 7: Custom node pack inference (`vibecomfy/porting/workbench.py`, `vibecomfy/node_packs_install.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Reuse `resolve_node_packs(...)` / `missing_packs_for_workflow(...)` concepts, but pass only runtime-relevant class types after helper filtering.
2. Include pack name, repo, pip packages, and matched classes in the port report.
3. Treat unresolved runtime class types as hard `port check` errors.
4. Treat known helper/UI classes as helper diagnostics, never as missing custom-node packs.

### Step 8: Model URL HEAD checks (`vibecomfy/porting/assets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add opt-in URL checking with `httpx.Client().head(..., follow_redirects=True)` and a deterministic timeout.
2. Report status code, final redirected URL, missing URL fields, duplicate asset targets, and license-gated/404 cases.
3. Do not download model bodies.
4. Keep HEAD checks disabled in ordinary tests, `validate`, `doctor`, and `run` unless `--head-check-models` is explicitly passed.

### Step 9: Positional widget alias detection and shared schema source (`vibecomfy/porting/widget_schema.py`, `vibecomfy/porting/widget_aliases.py`, `vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py`, `vibecomfy/workflow.py`, `tools/_compile_equivalence.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Move the committed widget schema and `resolve_widget_name(...)` into package code.
2. Update `normalize_to_api`, `VibeWorkflow.compile`, the package emitter, `tools/format_as_python.py`, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`, and `tools/_compile_equivalence.py` to use the same package-level widget schema/alias source.
3. Prefer schema-derived input order from `object_info` when available; fall back to the committed static widget schema when offline.
4. Add a diagnostic pass that compares compiled API inputs against schema inputs and reports unresolved `widget_N` inputs where a required named input is missing or where parity tooling would otherwise see false drift.
5. Move existing Wan/WanAnimate positional aliases out of private workflow-only state while preserving current behavior.

## Phase 4: CLI, Discoverability, and Live Validation Loop

### Step 10: Add explicit CLI entry points (`vibecomfy/commands/port.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Register `port` as an explicit command in `COMMANDS`.
2. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port check <workflow> [--json] [--head-check-models]` for no-write preflight.
3. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port convert <workflow> --out out/scratchpads/name.py [--json] [--head-check-models] [--ready-id kind/name]` for explicit Python output.
4. Make command help explain when to use `port check`, `port convert`, `doctor`, `validate`, `nodes install-plan`, and `--head-check-models`.
5. Return nonzero for hard errors: unresolved runtime class types, unresolved helper broadcasts, schema-required missing inputs, failed Python build, failed emitted scratchpad validation, or failed model HEAD checks when the user requested them.

### Step 11: Wire the live validation loop without making network checks mandatory (`vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/run.py`, `scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep existing `workflow.validate(schema_provider=...)` as the hard gate before queueing runtime prompts.
2. Add `doctor` guidance that points users to `port check` when failures involve unknown classes, missing required inputs, helper-node issues, or model assets.
3. Add a cheap port-report summary to RunPod corpus matrix logs and copy the emitted scratchpad/report next to existing convert/validate logs.
4. Keep model URL HEAD checks opt-in and out of normal `run` behavior.

### Step 12: Make discoverability first-class (`README.md`, `docs/authoring.md`, `docs/adding_templates_models.md`, `docs/errors_and_doctor.md`, `docs/template_porting_workbench.md`, `AGENTS.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a new docs page for the porting workbench with a direct import -> `port check` -> `port convert` -> `validate` / `doctor` -> optional RunPod loop.
2. Update authoring and template docs so humans and agents start with `port check` before manual template editing or RunPod validation.
3. Update failure/live-validation docs to map common failures to commands: schema issues to `port check`/`validate`, custom nodes to `nodes install-plan`, model URLs to `port check --head-check-models`, and Python materialization to `port convert`.
4. Update AGENTS.md or the synced skill guidance so agents know when to use the porting workbench.
5. Ensure command help text mirrors the docs so discoverability is present in both CLI and repository guidance.

## Phase 5: Tests and Battle Targets

### Step 13: Add focused unit and CLI tests (`tests/test_porting_workbench.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_workflow_core.py`, `tests/test_schema.py`, `tests/test_model_assets.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Test a simple fixture based on `image/z_image` or a tiny synthetic workflow: report shape, provenance, Python emission, import/build, compile, and validate.
2. Test `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_wan_animate_preprocess_kijai`: helper nodes are diagnosed separately, helper nodes do not survive compiled API, no compiled API link points to a removed helper node, required real node packs are suggested, and detection model assets are reported.
3. Test custom-node inference ignores known helper/UI nodes but still fails unresolved runtime classes.
4. Test API-shaped workflow model asset extraction, including filename-only assets with missing URL warnings.
5. Test widget alias diagnostics with a fake schema provider and keep existing Wan/WanAnimate alias expectations passing.
6. Test HEAD checks with injected/mocked HTTP behavior, not real network.
7. Add CLI smoke tests for `port check --json`, `port convert --out ... --json`, and command help discoverability.

### Step 14: Update parity and conversion tests (`tests/test_ready_templates.py`, `tools/_compile_equivalence.py`, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep ready-template parity tests passing after widget schema migration.
2. Verify `tools/convert_ready_templates.py --template ... --dry-run` still uses the moved emitter and reports equivalent diffs.
3. Add regression coverage that `vibecomfy convert` keeps its existing lightweight contract while `vibecomfy port convert` emits explicit Python.

## Execution Order
1. Add report and helper diagnostics first, because helper filtering changes the correctness of every downstream diagnostic.
2. Expand model asset extraction before wiring URL checks, so reports are complete for UI-shaped and API-shaped sources.
3. Move emitter/widget schema code with compatibility wrappers and parity tests in the same change set.
4. Add the workbench orchestrator and `port check` before adding `port convert` writes.
5. Add opt-in HEAD checks after offline report behavior is covered.
6. Wire `doctor` / RunPod summaries after the report shape is stable.
7. Finish with discoverability docs, AGENTS/skill guidance, and battle-target tests.

## Validation Order
1. Run focused porting tests first: `pytest -q tests/test_porting_workbench.py tests/test_model_assets.py tests/test_workflow_core.py tests/test_schema.py`.
2. Run CLI tests: `pytest -q tests/test_cli.py`.
3. Run ready-template and parity regressions: `pytest -q tests/test_ready_templates.py` plus `python -m tools.convert_ready_templates --template image/qwen_image_2512 --dry-run`.
4. Run node/model checks: `pytest -q tests/test_nodes_install.py tests/test_models_registry.py`.
5. Run the full local suite with `pytest`.
6. Manual/optional network check: `python -m vibecomfy.cli port check video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_wan_animate_preprocess_kijai --json --head-check-models`.

    "idea": "Build a holistic VibeComfy template porting workbench/toolset, based on the failures seen while porting production Reigh workflows to VibeComfy. The toolset should make it easy to find/import source Comfy workflows, convert them into proper Python ready-template/scratchpad format, preserve provenance, infer and validate custom node packs, infer and HEAD-check model asset URLs, resolve UI-only/helper nodes like Note/MarkdownNote/SetNode/GetNode and similar reroute/broadcast helpers, detect positional widget aliases by comparing compiled API prompts with Comfy object_info/schema, surface missing required inputs before a RunPod burn, emit actionable reports and tests, and support battle-testing against both a simple template and the current WanAnimate production-parity template. It should be pragmatic: build the first useful slice now, use it immediately on the current failures, and document the remaining roadmap. Avoid raw JSON execution as the steady-state path; workflows should land as Python-esque VibeComfy ready templates/scratchpads. Include a strategy for integrating this into the live validation loop so future users avoid the same URL/model/custom-node/schema failures.",
    "significant_flags": 8,
    "unresolved_flags": [
      {
        "id": "issue_hints",
        "concern": "Did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: issue_hints: Checked the current agent guidance surfaces and sync script. README tells agents to read `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md`, while `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` only checks AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, and `agents/openai.yaml`; its `LOCAL_COPY_TARGETS` entry for `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` is written by `--apply` but not checked by normal sync tests. The plan's wording of \"AGENTS.md or the synced skill guidance\" can therefore leave the README-directed skill stale unless the implementation explicitly updates or syncs that local copy.",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "scope",
        "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: scope: Checked model asset callers beyond the planned workbench. `vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py` and `vibecomfy/commands/fetch.py` both fall back to `extract_from_raw_workflow(load_workflow_json(path))`, and their warning/fetch paths assume every returned entry has a usable `url`. The revised plan expands `vibecomfy/model_assets.py` to infer API-shaped filename-only assets and mark missing URLs as warnings, but unless those warning-only candidates are kept out of the legacy extractor's return shape or all callers are updated, existing doctor/fetch behavior can break.",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "all_locations",
        "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: all_locations: Checked agent-skill supporting infrastructure. `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py` validates `AGENTS.md`, the `CLAUDE.md` symlink, OpenAI metadata, and `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` check mode, but it does not validate `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` freshness even though README directs agents there. Step 12 should explicitly include running `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py --apply` or adding a freshness check for that local copy if discoverability for agents is a must-have.",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "callers",
        "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: callers: Checked `vibecomfy/model_assets.py` callers. `entries_from_scratchpad_path` is currently used for structured ready-template requirements, while `extract_from_raw_workflow` returns only dicts with `name`, `url`, and `subdir`; `commands/fetch.py` and `commands/doctor.py` index `entry['url']`. If Step 3 changes `extract_from_raw_workflow` itself to return filename-only API candidates without URLs, those existing callers will need defensive handling or the new filename-only candidates should be emitted only through `vibecomfy.porting.assets`.",
        "category": "correctness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-004",
        "concern": "port-convert output mode: the plan extracts the current ready-template emitter for default scratchpad output, but the existing emitter assumes `READY_METADATA['ready_template']` and `source_type='ready_template'`; `port convert` needs an explicit scratchpad emission mode when `--ready-id` is absent.",
        "category": "correctness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-005",
        "concern": "agent discoverability sync: updating only AGENTS.md or OpenAI metadata may leave `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` stale even though README tells agents to read that local skill file.",
        "category": "completeness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-006",
        "concern": "model asset API extraction: adding filename-only API asset candidates to `extract_from_raw_workflow` can break existing doctor/fetch callers that assume every returned asset has a URL.",
        "category": "correctness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      },
      {
        "id": "correctness",
        "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: correctness: Checked `tools/format_as_python.py`; the current reusable emitter always emits `READY_METADATA`, `READY_REQUIREMENTS`, `WorkflowSource(... source_type=\"ready_template\")`, and reads `READY_METADATA[\"ready_template\"]`. The revised plan says default `port convert` examples should emit to `out/scratchpads/...` and only use `--ready-id` for ready-template-shaped output, but it does not explicitly require a scratchpad emission mode distinct from the ready-template emitter, so a naive extraction can mislabel scratchpads as ready templates or fail when no ready id is supplied.",
        "category": "correctness",
        "severity": "significant",
        "status": "open"
      }
    ],
    "resolved_flags": [
      {
        "id": "FLAG-001",
        "concern": "custom-node inference: `missing_packs_for_workflow` currently treats helper/UI nodes as unresolved class types; making unresolved classes a hard `port check` error will fail the WanAnimate target unless the workbench filters helper nodes or analyzes the compiled helper-stripped graph for pack inference.",
        "resolution": "Revised the plan to separate helper-node diagnostics from custom-node pack inference, cover API-shaped model asset extraction, preserve existing conversion caller contracts, include parity tooling in widget-schema migration, and add discoverability as a first-class CLI/docs/AGENTS requirement."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-002",
        "concern": "model asset coverage: the plan relies on `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)`, but that function returns no assets for API-shaped workflows, so API prompt sources and normalized workflows can produce incomplete model URL/provenance reports.",
        "resolution": "Revised the plan to separate helper-node diagnostics from custom-node pack inference, cover API-shaped model asset extraction, preserve existing conversion caller contracts, include parity tooling in widget-schema migration, and add discoverability as a first-class CLI/docs/AGENTS requirement."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-003",
        "concern": "widget alias migration: the plan omits `tools/_compile_equivalence.py` from the centralization list even though ready-template conversion uses it to compare compiled output and it imports `tools._widget_schema.resolve_widget_name`.",
        "resolution": "`tools/_compile_equivalence.py` imports `resolve_widget_name` from `tools._widget_schema`, and `tools/convert_ready_templates.py` imports `_widget_value_counter` from that equivalence module."
      },
      {
        "id": "correctness-1",
        "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: correctness: Ran `missing_packs_for_workflow(workflow_from_ready('video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_wan_animate_preprocess_kijai'))` against the current repo. It suggests the real packs, but also returns unresolved helper/UI classes including `GetNode`, `SetNode`, `MarkdownNote`, and `Note`; because Step 4 says unresolved classes are hard errors for `port check`, the planned WanAnimate battle target would fail before the helper-node resolution pass unless pack inference is run on the compiled/helper-stripped API or explicitly excludes known helper classes.",
        "resolution": "Revised the plan to separate helper-node diagnostics from custom-node pack inference, cover API-shaped model asset extraction, preserve existing conversion caller contracts, include parity tooling in widget-schema migration, and add discoverability as a first-class CLI/docs/AGENTS requirement."
      },
      {
        "id": "correctness-2",
        "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: correctness: Checked `vibecomfy/workflow.py` and confirmed compile-time helper handling already drops `Note`, `MarkdownNote`, `SetNode`, and `GetNode`, and rewrites resolvable Set/Get broadcasts into direct links. The plan's Step 6 is directionally consistent with existing behavior, but the diagnostic needs to inspect the unresolved cases before compile silently drops them; otherwise dangling helper references may be skipped rather than reported as errors.",
        "resolution": "Revised the plan to separate helper-node diagnostics from custom-node pack inference, cover API-shaped model asset extraction, preserve existing conversion caller contracts, include parity tooling in widget-schema migration, and add discoverability as a first-class CLI/docs/AGENTS requirement."
      }
    ],

  "criteria_check": {
    "count": 17,
    "items": [
      {
        "criterion": "`python -m vibecomfy.cli port check <workflow> --json` returns a structured report with source provenance, schema issues, custom-node suggestions, model assets, helper-node diagnostics, widget alias diagnostics, and recommended next commands.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_shell",
          "read_build_output"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`python -m vibecomfy.cli port convert <workflow> --out <path> --json` emits a Python scratchpad/ready-template module whose `build()` returns a `VibeWorkflow` without re-loading the original JSON at runtime.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_shell",
          "read_files",
          "read_build_output"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "The emitted Python module preserves provenance fields including original source path or id, source hash, detected shape, and ready id when supplied.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "read_files",
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Custom-node pack inference ignores known helper/UI nodes and still reports unresolved runtime class types as hard errors.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "read_build_output"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "The porting report flags unresolved `SetNode` / `GetNode` helper broadcasts before compile can silently drop them.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "The porting report extracts model asset candidates from UI-shaped workflows, API-shaped prompts, scratchpad metadata, and ready-template requirements, warning when URLs are missing.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "read_build_output"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "The porting report flags missing required schema inputs before runtime queueing for workflows validated with an available schema provider.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "WanAnimate helper nodes (`Note`, `MarkdownNote`, `SetNode`, `GetNode`) do not survive compiled API output, no compiled API link points to a removed helper node, and helper classes are not reported as unresolved custom-node packs.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "parse_diff"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Positional `widget_N` alias handling is centralized and all callers, including `normalize_to_api`, `VibeWorkflow.compile`, the Python emitter, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`, and `tools/_compile_equivalence.py`, use the shared source.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "parse_diff"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Model asset HEAD checks are opt-in, use injectable/mocked HTTP behavior in tests, and report status without downloading model bodies.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "parse_diff"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Existing `vibecomfy convert` tests still pass, while new `vibecomfy port convert` tests prove explicit Python materialization.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "parse_diff"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Focused tests for the simple template path and the WanAnimate production-parity path pass.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "CLI help and repository docs make `port check` discoverable as the recommended first preflight before manual template editing or RunPod validation.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_shell",
          "read_files",
          "read_build_output"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Existing local test suite passes with `pytest`.",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "The new porting modules keep CLI formatting separate from analysis logic and avoid duplicating node/model/schema inference code already present in the package.",
        "priority": "should",
        "requires": [
          "parse_diff",
          "subjective_judgment"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "The docs give future users and agents a short live validation loop that starts with `port check`, proceeds to Python scratchpad conversion, and only then spends RunPod time.",
        "priority": "should",
        "requires": [
          "read_files",
          "subjective_judgment"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Optional real network HEAD checks pass for the current model asset URLs.",
        "priority": "info",
        "requires": [

          "observe_runtime_logs"
        ]
      }
