The revised plan expands `vibecomfy/model_assets.py` to infer API-shaped filename-only assets and mark missing URLs as warnings, but unless those warning-only candidates are kept out of the legacy extractor's return shape or all callers are updated, existing doctor/fetch behavior can break.",
    "category": "completeness",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "open",
    "weight": null
  },
  {
    "id": "all_locations",
    "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: all_locations: Checked agent-skill supporting infrastructure. `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py` validates `AGENTS.md`, the `CLAUDE.md` symlink, OpenAI metadata, and `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` check mode, but it does not validate `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` freshness even though README directs agents there. Step 12 should explicitly include running `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py --apply` or adding a freshness check for that local copy if discoverability for agents is a must-have.",
    "evidence": "all_locations: Checked agent-skill supporting infrastructure. `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py` validates `AGENTS.md`, the `CLAUDE.md` symlink, OpenAI metadata, and `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` check mode, but it does not validate `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` freshness even though README directs agents there. Step 12 should explicitly include running `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py --apply` or adding a freshness check for that local copy if discoverability for agents is a must-have.",
    "category": "completeness",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "open",
    "weight": null
  },
  {
    "id": "callers",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: callers: Checked `vibecomfy/model_assets.py` callers. `entries_from_scratchpad_path` is currently used for structured ready-template requirements, while `extract_from_raw_workflow` returns only dicts with `name`, `url`, and `subdir`; `commands/fetch.py` and `commands/doctor.py` index `entry['url']`. If Step 3 changes `extract_from_raw_workflow` itself to return filename-only API candidates without URLs, those existing callers will need defensive handling or the new filename-only candidates should be emitted only through `vibecomfy.porting.assets`.",
    "evidence": "callers: Checked `vibecomfy/model_assets.py` callers. `entries_from_scratchpad_path` is currently used for structured ready-template requirements, while `extract_from_raw_workflow` returns only dicts with `name`, `url`, and `subdir`; `commands/fetch.py` and `commands/doctor.py` index `entry['url']`. If Step 3 changes `extract_from_raw_workflow` itself to return filename-only API candidates without URLs, those existing callers will need defensive handling or the new filename-only candidates should be emitted only through `vibecomfy.porting.assets`.",

  {
    "id": "FLAG-004",
    "concern": "port-convert output mode: the plan extracts the current ready-template emitter for default scratchpad output, but the existing emitter assumes `READY_METADATA['ready_template']` and `source_type='ready_template'`; `port convert` needs an explicit scratchpad emission mode when `--ready-id` is absent.",
    "evidence": "`tools/format_as_python.py` always emits `READY_METADATA`, `READY_REQUIREMENTS`, `WorkflowSource(... source_type=\"ready_template\")`, and references `READY_METADATA[\"ready_template\"]`, while the revised plan defaults `port convert` examples to `out/scratchpads/...` and treats `--ready-id` as optional.",
    "category": "correctness",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "open",
    "weight": null
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-005",
    "concern": "agent discoverability sync: updating only AGENTS.md or OpenAI metadata may leave `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` stale even though README tells agents to read that local skill file.",
    "evidence": "`scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` writes `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` only in `--apply` via `LOCAL_COPY_TARGETS`, but `check()` and `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py` do not validate that local copy; README points setup step 4 to `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md`.",
    "category": "completeness",

    "status": "open",
    "weight": null

    "evidence": "`commands/fetch.py` and `commands/doctor.py` both call `extract_from_raw_workflow(load_workflow_json(path))` and then use `entry['url']`; the revised plan says API-shaped assets inferred from filenames without URLs should be warnings.",
    "category": "correctness",
    "severity": "significant",

  {
    "id": "correctness",

The revised plan says default `port convert` examples should emit to `out/scratchpads/...` and only use `--ready-id` for ready-template-shaped output, but it does not explicitly require a scratchpad emission mode distinct from the ready-template emitter, so a naive extraction can mislabel scratchpads as ready templates or fail when no ready id is supplied.",

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan places _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ but also needs vibeworkflow.connect in vibecomfy/workflow.py to consult handle_trace_active and record events. because importing vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log executes vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py, and that package currently imports vibeworkflow at top level, any top-level import from workflow.py to vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log would create a circular import risk unless the implementation uses carefully lazy imports or moves the shared trace module outside the blocks package.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "mp-2-implement-p1-hard-gates-20260426-1845"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the op-side backfill assumption against `tests/snapshots/qwen_image_edit.api.json`, `tests/snapshots/flux2_klein_9b_gguf_t2i.api.json`, and `tests/snapshots/flux2_klein_4b_image_edit_distilled.api.json`. only `flux2_klein_4b_t2i` proves a uuid subgraph accepts a `text` input; the qwen edit uuid currently exposes only `image`, the flux 9b uuid exposes no inputs, and the flux edit uuids expose only image inputs, so wiring a new text encoder into `text` is an unverified runtime contract likely to fail without a uuid-specific input map or schema-backed validation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-012",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `textencodeqwenimageedit` usage in the current repo and snapshots. the class is listed in `prompt_node_classes` and has metadata tests, but no snapshotted ready template shows its output wired into the qwen uuid subgraph, so the plan's qwen backfill may connect a conditioning/text-encoder output to an input name/type the subgraph does not actually accept.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-016",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: prompt-bearing route coverage: inspected tests/snapshots/flux2_klein_9b_gguf_t2i.api.json and found only a uuid subgraph node with empty inputs plus saveimage. v4 keeps the `image.t2i(..., model='flux2_klein_9b_gguf')` router rule but only says to 'read snapshot to identify' a primitive or cliptextencode prompt node; the actual snapshot has neither, so that t2i route cannot honor the caller's prompt while preserving snapshot parity.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: verb-native edit contract: compared the brief's `image.edit(image, instruction, ...)` requirement with v3's qwen/flux edit behavior. v3 documents both snapshotted edit templates as image-only and says `instruction=` is ignored with a warning, which is an explicit divergence from the requested instruction-driven edit api rather than a full implementation of that verb.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-006",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the proposed _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ is supporting infrastructure that both blocks and workflow need. given the current package dependency direction (blocks imports workflow), the safer location is a neutral module such as vibecomfy/handles.py or vibecomfy/handle_warnings.py; leaving it under blocks increases import-order fragility unless the implementation is very deliberate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "mp-2-implement-p1-hard-gates-20260426-1845"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-020",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: checked the proposed backfill helper integration locations. v5 adds `vibecomfy/ops/_backfill.py` and tests op-mutated workflows in `tests/test_ops.py`, but it does not add any schema/object-info support or hardcoded uuid input map for the three opaque subgraphs it mutates, which is supporting infrastructure needed to make the new text-edge insertion reliable rather than merely syntactically present in compiled api json.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "edit-branch-selection": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-022",
      "concern": "edit branch selection: `image.edit(..., model='flux2_klein_4b')` targets a snapshot with two loadimage nodes and two uuid/saveimage branches, but the plan does not define which image input, subgraph, or output is primary for op mutation and artifact selection.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: for the block helper caller path, vibeworkflow.connect is usually called by vibecomfy.blocks._utils.connect, not by the user's block function directly. the plan's immediate-caller-frame heuristic can work if _utils.connect has a handle local named source, but it will not identify the higher-level block function unless record_raw_ref_coercion preserves or walks to the block frame rather than skipping blocks frames.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "mp-2-implement-p1-hard-gates-20260426-1845"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: set_prompt callers: v4 removes set_prompt calls for edit templates by deferring image.edit, which avoids calling set_input against non-existent instruction fields. however, the remaining flux2_klein_9b_gguf_t2i t2i route still has no visible prompt-bearing field in its snapshot, so `image.t2i(prompt, model='flux2_klein_9b_gguf')` will either ignore the prompt or require a non-parity graph change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-017",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the planned `image.edit(image, instruction, model=...)` callers against the two edit snapshots. `qwen_image_edit` has one loadimage feeding uuid 102, while `flux2_klein_4b_image_edit_distilled` has two loadimage nodes and two uuid/saveimage branches; the plan says to write the caller image to `loadimage` if present and to find the terminal subgraph, but it does not define which loadimage/subgraph/output is primary for the multi-branch flux edit template, so `image.edit(..., model='flux2_klein_4b')` may mutate the wrong branch or leave one branch with stale fixture images.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `set_prompt` callers after v5's backfill change. for templates that already register a prompt (`z_image`, `flux2_klein_4b_t2i`, wan/ltx via prompt encoders), `wf.set_prompt(...)` has a clear target; for the three backfilled uuid templates, the caller path only works if the newly connected `text` input is accepted by the subgraph, which remains the same unverified runtime assumption flagged under correctness.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    }
  ],

      ]

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the plan's record_raw_ref_coercion helper says it finds the first non-vibecomfy/blocks frame for the site. that conflicts with the stated goal that raw string refs in block code are visible: a missed handles(image=\"...\") inside a block called by a ready template could be attributed to an allowlisted ready_templates frame and suppressed, or at minimum would not identify the block module as the source.",

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: convert command interaction: checked current convert.py and scratchpad_loader.render_scratchpad. v4 extends resolve_workflow_path so ready-template ids resolve to `.py` files, while convert keeps using resolve_workflow_path and sets `source_is_path=true`; render_scratchpad then emits `workflow_from_file('/.../ready_templates/...py')`, but workflow_from_file expects json and will fail on a python ready template. this is a new regression risk for converting ready-template ids.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
Build a holistic VibeComfy template porting workbench/toolset, based on the failures seen while porting production Reigh workflows to VibeComfy. The toolset should make it easy to find/import source Comfy workflows, convert them into proper Python ready-template/scratchpad format, preserve provenance, infer and validate custom node packs, infer and HEAD-check model asset URLs, resolve UI-only/helper nodes like Note/MarkdownNote/SetNode/GetNode and similar reroute/broadcast helpers, detect positional widget aliases by comparing compiled API prompts with Comfy object_info/schema, surface missing required inputs before a RunPod burn, emit actionable reports and tests, and support battle-testing against both a simple template and the current WanAnimate production-parity template. It should be pragmatic: build the first useful slice now, use it immediately on the current failures, and document the remaining roadmap. Avoid raw JSON execution as the steady-state path; workflows should land as Python-esque VibeComfy ready templates/scratchpads. Include a strategy for integrating this into the live validation loop so future users avoid the same URL/model/custom-node/schema failures.

The VibeComfy porting toolset must make it obvious to humans and agents when to use it: command names/help, README/authoring docs, AGENTS.md or skill guidance, and failure/live-validation docs should all point to port check before manual template editing or expensive RunPod validation. The tool should document when to use check, convert, doctor/validate, model URL checks, custom-node pack discovery, and Python-template conversion.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: VibeComfy Template Porting Workbench

## Overview
The new critique still does not show that the workbench is aimed at the wrong subsystem or root cause. The correct target remains a first-class `vibecomfy port ...` workflow over the existing conversion, schema, node-pack, model-asset, and ready-template machinery. The refinements are about preserving contracts at the edges: `port convert` needs a true scratchpad output mode distinct from ready-template output, filename-only API asset discovery must not change the legacy URL-bearing `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` contract used by `doctor` and `fetch`, and agent discoverability must update the README-directed local skill copy as well as AGENTS/OpenAI guidance.

The first useful slice is a package-level `vibecomfy.porting` workbench plus a discoverable CLI surface. `port check` should be the obvious cheap preflight before manual Python-template editing or expensive RunPod validation. `port convert` should emit Python-esque VibeWorkflow scratchpads by default, and ready-template-shaped modules only when `--ready-id` is supplied. Existing `vibecomfy convert`, `doctor`, `fetch`, and ready-template materialization contracts should remain compatible unless they explicitly opt into the new workbench APIs.

## Phase 1: Foundation - Report Shape, Source Loading, and Helper Semantics

### Step 1: Add a porting report model (`vibecomfy/porting/report.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Define dataclasses for `PortReport`, `PortIssue`, `PortArtifact`, `NodePackSuggestion`, `AssetCandidate`, and `AssetCheckResult` with stable JSON serialization.
2. Include source id/path, detected workflow shape, source hash, normalized API node count, emitted Python path, output mode (`scratchpad` or `ready_template`), helper-node diagnostics, custom-node suggestions, unresolved runtime class types, model asset candidates, schema issues, widget alias observations, and recommended next commands.
3. Keep issue severities aligned with current validation concepts: `error`, `warning`, and `info`.

### Step 2: Centralize helper/UI-node classification (`vibecomfy/porting/helpers.py`, `vibecomfy/workflow.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Move the helper-node class set and helper-resolution diagnostics into a reusable module that knows about `Note`, `MarkdownNote`, `SetNode`, and `GetNode`.
2. Add a diagnostic pass that inspects the original IR before compile and reports unresolved `GetNode` / `SetNode` broadcasts instead of letting compile silently skip dangling helper references.
3. Expose a helper-stripped runtime class view for downstream diagnostics so custom-node pack inference does not treat `Note`, `MarkdownNote`, `SetNode`, or `GetNode` as missing installable node packs.
4. Keep `VibeWorkflow.compile("api")` behavior compatible: resolved helper broadcasts still rewrite to direct links; helper nodes still do not survive into API prompts.

### Step 3: Add porting-only model asset analysis without changing legacy extractor contracts (`vibecomfy/porting/assets.py`, `vibecomfy/model_assets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` returning only URL-bearing dictionaries with `name`, `url`, and `subdir`, so existing `doctor` and `fetch` callers remain safe.
2. Add a new porting-only analyzer that merges URL-bearing assets from UI workflow `properties.models`, normalized API prompts, loaded scratchpad/ready-template metadata, and `READY_REQUIREMENTS`.
3. Add API-shaped filename discovery in the porting analyzer as `AssetCandidate` records with optional `url`, not as return values from `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)`.
4. Mark filename-only candidates without URLs as actionable warnings in the port report rather than passing them to legacy fetch/download code.
5. Factor shared subdir/name normalization helpers only if useful, while preserving the public behavior of `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` and `entries_from_scratchpad_path(...)`.

## Phase 2: Python Emission and Compatibility Boundaries

### Step 4: Move reusable emission primitives without forcing ready-template semantics (`vibecomfy/porting/emitter.py`, `tools/format_as_python.py`, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Extract reusable `_node(...)`-style emission primitives from `tools/format_as_python.py` into package code.
2. Preserve explicit node IDs, deterministic ordering, `_extras` for non-identifier inputs, registered input handling, and current override behavior.
3. Split package emission into two explicit modes:
   - `emit_scratchpad_python(...)`: default for `port convert`, emits `PORT_METADATA` or equivalent provenance, uses `WorkflowSource(... source_type="scratchpad")`, and does not require `READY_METADATA["ready_template"]`.
   - `emit_ready_template_python(...)`: used only when `--ready-id` is supplied or by ready-template tooling, emits `READY_METADATA`, `READY_REQUIREMENTS`, and `WorkflowSource(... source_type="ready_template")`.
4. Keep `tools/format_as_python.py` and `tools/convert_ready_templates.py` as compatibility wrappers around the ready-template emission mode.
5. Do not change the established `vibecomfy convert` contract in `vibecomfy/commands/convert.py`; the new explicit Python materialization behavior belongs to `vibecomfy port convert` first.

### Step 5: Preserve both conversion contracts (`vibecomfy/scratchpad_loader.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/convert.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/port.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Leave existing lightweight scratchpad rendering available for compatibility.
2. Implement `port convert` without `--ready-id` as true scratchpad emission: `build()` returns a `VibeWorkflow` with scratchpad provenance and no ready-template metadata requirement.
3. Implement `port convert --ready-id kind/name` as ready-template candidate emission: `build()` returns a `VibeWorkflow` with ready-template metadata and structured requirements.
4. Default examples and docs to `out/scratchpads/...` so promotion to `ready_templates/` remains intentional.
5. Validate emitted Python by importing it, calling `build()`, compiling API, and running schema validation when available.

## Phase 3: Diagnostics - Catch Current Failure Classes Before RunPod

### Step 6: Add the workbench orchestrator (`vibecomfy/porting/workbench.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Implement `analyze_source(...)` to load ready ids, scratchpads, raw JSON paths, and indexed workflow references through existing loader/normalizer paths.
2. Preserve provenance in the report and emitted metadata: source path/id, source hash, original shape, source workflow id when known, converter version, output mode, and whether schema was available.
3. Run helper diagnostics before custom-node inference.
4. Run custom-node pack inference against the helper-stripped runtime class view, while reporting unresolved helper broadcasts separately as helper errors.
5. Run the porting-only model asset analyzer so filename-only API candidates become warnings without breaking legacy model asset callers.
6. Reuse `workflow.validate(schema_provider=...)` for schema issues and include formatted `missing_required_input`, `unknown_input`, `unknown_class_type`, `invalid_link_shape`, and type mismatch results in the report.

### Step 7: Custom node pack inference (`vibecomfy/porting/workbench.py`, `vibecomfy/node_packs_install.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Reuse `resolve_node_packs(...)` / `missing_packs_for_workflow(...)` concepts, but pass only runtime-relevant class types after helper filtering.
2. Include pack name, repo, pip packages, and matched classes in the port report.
3. Treat unresolved runtime class types as hard `port check` errors.
4. Treat known helper/UI classes as helper diagnostics, never as missing custom-node packs.

### Step 8: Model URL HEAD checks (`vibecomfy/porting/assets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add opt-in URL checking for asset candidates that have URLs, using `httpx.Client().head(..., follow_redirects=True)` and a deterministic timeout.
2. Report status code, final redirected URL, missing URL fields, duplicate asset targets, and license-gated/404 cases.
3. Do not download model bodies.
4. Keep HEAD checks disabled in ordinary tests, `validate`, `doctor`, `fetch`, and `run` unless `--head-check-models` is explicitly passed through `port check` or `port convert`.

### Step 9: Positional widget alias detection and shared schema source (`vibecomfy/porting/widget_schema.py`, `vibecomfy/porting/widget_aliases.py`, `vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py`, `vibecomfy/workflow.py`, `tools/_compile_equivalence.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Move the committed widget schema and `resolve_widget_name(...)` into package code.
2. Update `normalize_to_api`, `VibeWorkflow.compile`, the package emitter, `tools/format_as_python.py`, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`, and `tools/_compile_equivalence.py` to use the same package-level widget schema/alias source.
3. Prefer schema-derived input order from `object_info` when available; fall back to the committed static widget schema when offline.
4. Add a diagnostic pass that compares compiled API inputs against schema inputs and reports unresolved `widget_N` inputs where a required named input is missing or where parity tooling would otherwise see false drift.
5. Move existing Wan/WanAnimate positional aliases out of private workflow-only state while preserving current behavior.

## Phase 4: CLI, Discoverability, and Live Validation Loop

### Step 10: Add explicit CLI entry points (`vibecomfy/commands/port.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Register `port` as an explicit command in `COMMANDS`.
2. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port check <workflow> [--json] [--head-check-models]` for no-write preflight.
3. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port convert <workflow> --out out/scratchpads/name.py [--json] [--head-check-models]` for scratchpad Python output.
4. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port convert <workflow> --out ready_templates/kind/name.py --ready-id kind/name [--json]` for ready-template candidate output.
5. Make command help explain when to use `port check`, `port convert`, `doctor`, `validate`, `nodes install-plan`, `fetch`, and `--head-check-models`.
6. Return nonzero for hard errors: unresolved runtime class types, unresolved helper broadcasts, schema-required missing inputs, failed Python build, failed emitted scratchpad/template validation, or failed model HEAD checks when the user requested them.

### Step 11: Wire the live validation loop without making network checks mandatory (`vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/run.py`, `scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep existing `workflow.validate(schema_provider=...)` as the hard gate before queueing runtime prompts.
2. Add `doctor` guidance that points users to `port check` when failures involve unknown classes, missing required inputs, helper-node issues, or model assets.
3. Add a cheap port-report summary to RunPod corpus matrix logs and copy the emitted scratchpad/report next to existing convert/validate logs.
4. Keep model URL HEAD checks opt-in and out of normal `run`, `doctor`, and `fetch` behavior.

### Step 12: Make discoverability first-class and sync every agent guidance surface (`README.md`, `docs/authoring.md`, `docs/adding_templates_models.md`, `docs/errors_and_doctor.md`, `docs/template_porting_workbench.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md`, `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py`, `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a new docs page for the porting workbench with a direct import -> `port check` -> `port convert` -> `validate` / `doctor` -> optional RunPod loop.
2. Update authoring and template docs so humans and agents start with `port check` before manual template editing or RunPod validation.
3. Update failure/live-validation docs to map common failures to commands: schema issues to `port check`/`validate`, custom nodes to `nodes install-plan`, model URLs to `port check --head-check-models`, Python materialization to `port convert`, and downloads to `fetch` only when URL-bearing assets are available.
4. Update the source agent guidance that feeds AGENTS/OpenAI/Claude-facing copies, then run `python scripts/sync_agent_skill.py --apply` so `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` is refreshed.
5. Extend `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` check mode or `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py` so the README-directed `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` local copy is checked for freshness.
6. Ensure command help text mirrors the docs so discoverability is present in both CLI and repository guidance.

## Phase 5: Tests and Battle Targets

### Step 13: Add focused unit and CLI tests (`tests/test_porting_workbench.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_workflow_core.py`, `tests/test_schema.py`, `tests/test_model_assets.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Test a simple fixture based on `image/z_image` or a tiny synthetic workflow: report shape, provenance, scratchpad Python emission, import/build, compile, and validate.
2. Test `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_wan_animate_preprocess_kijai`: helper nodes are diagnosed separately, helper nodes do not survive compiled API, no compiled API link points to a removed helper node, required real node packs are suggested, and detection model assets are reported.
3. Test custom-node inference ignores known helper/UI nodes but still fails unresolved runtime classes.
4. Test porting-only API-shaped workflow model asset candidates, including filename-only assets with missing URL warnings, without changing `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` return shape.
5. Test widget alias diagnostics with a fake schema provider and keep existing Wan/WanAnimate alias expectations passing.
6. Test HEAD checks with injected/mocked HTTP behavior, not real network.
7. Add CLI smoke tests for `port check --json`, scratchpad-mode `port convert --out ... --json`, ready-template-mode `port convert --ready-id ...`, and command help discoverability.

### Step 14: Update parity, conversion, and sync tests (`tests/test_ready_templates.py`, `tools/_compile_equivalence.py`, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`, `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep ready-template parity tests passing after widget schema migration.
2. Verify `tools/convert_ready_templates.py --template ... --dry-run` still uses the moved ready-template emitter and reports equivalent diffs.
3. Add regression coverage that `vibecomfy convert` keeps its existing lightweight contract while `vibecomfy port convert` emits explicit scratchpad Python by default.
4. Add regression coverage that ready-template emission is only used by `port convert --ready-id` and ready-template tooling.
5. Add freshness coverage for `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` or the sync path that regenerates it.

## Execution Order
1. Add report and helper diagnostics first, because helper filtering changes the correctness of every downstream diagnostic.
2. Add porting-only model asset analysis while preserving legacy `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` behavior for `doctor` and `fetch`.
3. Split emitter code into explicit scratchpad and ready-template modes before wiring `port convert`.
4. Move widget schema code with compatibility wrappers and parity tests in the same change set.
5. Add the workbench orchestrator and `port check` before adding `port convert` writes.
6. Add opt-in HEAD checks after offline report behavior is covered.
7. Wire `doctor` / RunPod summaries after the report shape is stable.
8. Finish with discoverability docs, synced agent guidance, and battle-target tests.

## Validation Order
1. Run focused porting tests first: `pytest -q tests/test_porting_workbench.py tests/test_model_assets.py tests/test_workflow_core.py tests/test_schema.py`.
2. Run CLI tests: `pytest -q tests/test_cli.py`.
3. Run ready-template and parity regressions: `pytest -q tests/test_ready_templates.py` plus `python -m tools.convert_ready_templates --template image/qwen_image_2512 --dry-run`.
4. Run agent guidance sync tests: `python scripts/sync_agent_skill.py --check` and `pytest -q tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py`.
5. Run node/model checks: `pytest -q tests/test_nodes_install.py tests/test_models_registry.py`.
6. Run the full local suite with `pytest`.
7. Manual/optional network check: `python -m vibecomfy.cli port check video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_wan_animate_preprocess_kijai --json --head-check-models`.

The revised validation order should either use `python scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` or include a small script change adding `--check`, otherwise the prescribed validation command will fail before tests run.",
    "evidence": "all_locations: Checked `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py --help` and found no `--check` option, only `--apply`; default execution is check mode. The revised validation order should either use `python scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` or include a small script change adding `--check`, otherwise the prescribed validation command will fail before tests run.",
    "category": "completeness",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "open",
    "weight": null
  },
  {
    "id": "correctness",
    "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: correctness: Checked the widget mapping plan against the current `tools/_widget_schema.py` contract. That file explicitly says positional widget schema omits link-only inputs and includes only widget-type inputs, but the revised plan still says to prefer schema-derived input order from `object_info` without spelling out filtering of link-only inputs such as MODEL/CLIP/IMAGE. Because `vibecomfy/schema/provider.py` represents all object_info inputs together and does not currently mark whether an input is a UI widget, a naive object_info order can reintroduce positional shifts instead of detecting them.",
    "evidence": "correctness: Checked the widget mapping plan against the current `tools/_widget_schema.py` contract. That file explicitly says positional widget schema omits link-only inputs and includes only widget-type inputs, but the revised plan still says to prefer schema-derived input order from `object_info` without spelling out filtering of link-only inputs such as MODEL/CLIP/IMAGE. Because `vibecomfy/schema/provider.py` represents all object_info inputs together and does not currently mark whether an input is a UI widget, a naive object_info order can reintroduce positional shifts instead of detecting them.",
    "category": "correctness",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "open",
    "weight": null
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-008",
    "concern": "widget alias schema source: preferring raw object_info input order for `widgets_values` is underspecified because object_info includes link-only inputs as well as UI widget inputs, while positional widget mapping must use widget-only order.",
    "evidence": "`tools/_widget_schema.py` documents that link-only inputs are not included because only widget-type inputs count for positional indexing; `vibecomfy/schema/provider.py` flattens object_info inputs into a single `inputs` dict without an explicit widget/link-position marker.",
    "category": "correctness",
    "severity": "significant",
    "status": "open",
    "weight": null
  }

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan places _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ but also needs vibeworkflow.connect in vibecomfy/workflow.py to consult handle_trace_active and record events. because importing vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log executes vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py, and that package currently imports vibeworkflow at top level, any top-level import from workflow.py to vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log would create a circular import risk unless the implementation uses carefully lazy imports or moves the shared trace module outside the blocks package.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "mp-2-implement-p1-hard-gates-20260426-1845"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the op-side backfill assumption against `tests/snapshots/qwen_image_edit.api.json`, `tests/snapshots/flux2_klein_9b_gguf_t2i.api.json`, and `tests/snapshots/flux2_klein_4b_image_edit_distilled.api.json`. only `flux2_klein_4b_t2i` proves a uuid subgraph accepts a `text` input; the qwen edit uuid currently exposes only `image`, the flux 9b uuid exposes no inputs, and the flux edit uuids expose only image inputs, so wiring a new text encoder into `text` is an unverified runtime contract likely to fail without a uuid-specific input map or schema-backed validation.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "finish-higher-abstraction"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-012",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `textencodeqwenimageedit` usage in the current repo and snapshots. the class is listed in `prompt_node_classes` and has metadata tests, but no snapshotted ready template shows its output wired into the qwen uuid subgraph, so the plan's qwen backfill may connect a conditioning/text-encoder output to an input name/type the subgraph does not actually accept.",

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Idea:
Build a holistic VibeComfy template porting workbench/toolset, based on the failures seen while porting production Reigh workflows to VibeComfy. The toolset should make it easy to find/import source Comfy workflows, convert them into proper Python ready-template/scratchpad format, preserve provenance, infer and validate custom node packs, infer and HEAD-check model asset URLs, resolve UI-only/helper nodes like Note/MarkdownNote/SetNode/GetNode and similar reroute/broadcast helpers, detect positional widget aliases by comparing compiled API prompts with Comfy object_info/schema, surface missing required inputs before a RunPod burn, emit actionable reports and tests, and support battle-testing against both a simple template and the current WanAnimate production-parity template. It should be pragmatic: build the first useful slice now, use it immediately on the current failures, and document the remaining roadmap. Avoid raw JSON execution as the steady-state path; workflows should land as Python-esque VibeComfy ready templates/scratchpads. Include a strategy for integrating this into the live validation loop so future users avoid the same URL/model/custom-node/schema failures.

User notes and answers:
- User added: discoverability is a first-class requirement. The VibeComfy porting toolset must make it obvious to humans and agents when to use it: command names/help, README/authoring docs, AGENTS.md or skill guidance, and failure/live-validation docs should all point to port check before manual template editing or expensive RunPod validation. The tool should document when to use check, convert, doctor/validate, model URL checks, custom-node pack discovery, and Python-template conversion.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: VibeComfy Template Porting Workbench

## Overview
The remaining critique still does not show that the workbench is aimed at the wrong subsystem or root cause. The correct target remains a first-class `vibecomfy port ...` workflow over the existing conversion, schema, node-pack, model-asset, and ready-template machinery. The final refinements are execution details: use the actual default check mode for `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py`, and keep positional widget mapping based on widget-only order rather than raw `object_info` input order, because `object_info` includes link-only inputs that do not correspond to `widgets_values` positions.

The first useful slice is a package-level `vibecomfy.porting` workbench plus a discoverable CLI surface. `port check` should be the obvious cheap preflight before manual Python-template editing or expensive RunPod validation. `port convert` should emit Python-esque VibeWorkflow scratchpads by default, and ready-template-shaped modules only when `--ready-id` is supplied. Existing `vibecomfy convert`, `doctor`, `fetch`, and ready-template materialization contracts should remain compatible unless they explicitly opt into the new workbench APIs.

## Phase 1: Foundation - Report Shape, Source Loading, and Helper Semantics

### Step 1: Add a porting report model (`vibecomfy/porting/report.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Define dataclasses for `PortReport`, `PortIssue`, `PortArtifact`, `NodePackSuggestion`, `AssetCandidate`, and `AssetCheckResult` with stable JSON serialization.
2. Include source id/path, detected workflow shape, source hash, normalized API node count, emitted Python path, output mode (`scratchpad` or `ready_template`), helper-node diagnostics, custom-node suggestions, unresolved runtime class types, model asset candidates, schema issues, widget alias observations, and recommended next commands.
3. Keep issue severities aligned with current validation concepts: `error`, `warning`, and `info`.

### Step 2: Centralize helper/UI-node classification (`vibecomfy/porting/helpers.py`, `vibecomfy/workflow.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Move the helper-node class set and helper-resolution diagnostics into a reusable module that knows about `Note`, `MarkdownNote`, `SetNode`, and `GetNode`.
2. Add a diagnostic pass that inspects the original IR before compile and reports unresolved `GetNode` / `SetNode` broadcasts instead of letting compile silently skip dangling helper references.
3. Expose a helper-stripped runtime class view for downstream diagnostics so custom-node pack inference does not treat `Note`, `MarkdownNote`, `SetNode`, or `GetNode` as missing installable node packs.
4. Keep `VibeWorkflow.compile("api")` behavior compatible: resolved helper broadcasts still rewrite to direct links; helper nodes still do not survive into API prompts.

### Step 3: Add porting-only model asset analysis without changing legacy extractor contracts (`vibecomfy/porting/assets.py`, `vibecomfy/model_assets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` returning only URL-bearing dictionaries with `name`, `url`, and `subdir`, so existing `doctor` and `fetch` callers remain safe.
2. Add a new porting-only analyzer that merges URL-bearing assets from UI workflow `properties.models`, normalized API prompts, loaded scratchpad/ready-template metadata, and `READY_REQUIREMENTS`.
3. Add API-shaped filename discovery in the porting analyzer as `AssetCandidate` records with optional `url`, not as return values from `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)`.
4. Mark filename-only candidates without URLs as actionable warnings in the port report rather than passing them to legacy fetch/download code.
5. Factor shared subdir/name normalization helpers only if useful, while preserving the public behavior of `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` and `entries_from_scratchpad_path(...)`.

## Phase 2: Python Emission and Compatibility Boundaries

### Step 4: Move reusable emission primitives without forcing ready-template semantics (`vibecomfy/porting/emitter.py`, `tools/format_as_python.py`, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Extract reusable `_node(...)`-style emission primitives from `tools/format_as_python.py` into package code.
2. Preserve explicit node IDs, deterministic ordering, `_extras` for non-identifier inputs, registered input handling, and current override behavior.
3. Split package emission into two explicit modes:
   - `emit_scratchpad_python(...)`: default for `port convert`, emits `PORT_METADATA` or equivalent provenance, uses `WorkflowSource(... source_type="scratchpad")`, and does not require `READY_METADATA["ready_template"]`.
   - `emit_ready_template_python(...)`: used only when `--ready-id` is supplied or by ready-template tooling, emits `READY_METADATA`, `READY_REQUIREMENTS`, and `WorkflowSource(... source_type="ready_template")`.
4. Keep `tools/format_as_python.py` and `tools/convert_ready_templates.py` as compatibility wrappers around the ready-template emission mode.
5. Do not change the established `vibecomfy convert` contract in `vibecomfy/commands/convert.py`; the new explicit Python materialization behavior belongs to `vibecomfy port convert` first.

### Step 5: Preserve both conversion contracts (`vibecomfy/scratchpad_loader.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/convert.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/port.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Leave existing lightweight scratchpad rendering available for compatibility.
2. Implement `port convert` without `--ready-id` as true scratchpad emission: `build()` returns a `VibeWorkflow` with scratchpad provenance and no ready-template metadata requirement.
3. Implement `port convert --ready-id kind/name` as ready-template candidate emission: `build()` returns a `VibeWorkflow` with ready-template metadata and structured requirements.
4. Default examples and docs to `out/scratchpads/...` so promotion to `ready_templates/` remains intentional.
5. Validate emitted Python by importing it, calling `build()`, compiling API, and running schema validation when available.

## Phase 3: Diagnostics - Catch Current Failure Classes Before RunPod

### Step 6: Add the workbench orchestrator (`vibecomfy/porting/workbench.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Implement `analyze_source(...)` to load ready ids, scratchpads, raw JSON paths, and indexed workflow references through existing loader/normalizer paths.
2. Preserve provenance in the report and emitted metadata: source path/id, source hash, original shape, source workflow id when known, converter version, output mode, and whether schema was available.
3. Run helper diagnostics before custom-node inference.
4. Run custom-node pack inference against the helper-stripped runtime class view, while reporting unresolved helper broadcasts separately as helper errors.
5. Run the porting-only model asset analyzer so filename-only API candidates become warnings without breaking legacy model asset callers.
6. Reuse `workflow.validate(schema_provider=...)` for schema issues and include formatted `missing_required_input`, `unknown_input`, `unknown_class_type`, `invalid_link_shape`, and type mismatch results in the report.

### Step 7: Custom node pack inference (`vibecomfy/porting/workbench.py`, `vibecomfy/node_packs_install.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Reuse `resolve_node_packs(...)` / `missing_packs_for_workflow(...)` concepts, but pass only runtime-relevant class types after helper filtering.
2. Include pack name, repo, pip packages, and matched classes in the port report.
3. Treat unresolved runtime class types as hard `port check` errors.
4. Treat known helper/UI classes as helper diagnostics, never as missing custom-node packs.

### Step 8: Model URL HEAD checks (`vibecomfy/porting/assets.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add opt-in URL checking for asset candidates that have URLs, using `httpx.Client().head(..., follow_redirects=True)` and a deterministic timeout.
2. Report status code, final redirected URL, missing URL fields, duplicate asset targets, and license-gated/404 cases.
3. Do not download model bodies.
4. Keep HEAD checks disabled in ordinary tests, `validate`, `doctor`, `fetch`, and `run` unless `--head-check-models` is explicitly passed through `port check` or `port convert`.

### Step 9: Positional widget alias detection and widget-only schema source (`vibecomfy/porting/widget_schema.py`, `vibecomfy/porting/widget_aliases.py`, `vibecomfy/ingest/normalize.py`, `vibecomfy/workflow.py`, `tools/_compile_equivalence.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Move the committed widget-only schema and `resolve_widget_name(...)` into package code.
2. Preserve the current contract that positional widget schemas omit link-only inputs such as `MODEL`, `CLIP`, `IMAGE`, `VAE`, `LATENT`, and other socket inputs; only UI widget inputs may participate in `widgets_values` positional indexing.
3. Update `normalize_to_api`, `VibeWorkflow.compile`, the package emitter, `tools/format_as_python.py`, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`, and `tools/_compile_equivalence.py` to use the same package-level widget-only schema/alias source.
4. Do not derive positional widget order from raw `object_info` input order unless the provider exposes enough metadata to distinguish widget inputs from link-only socket inputs. Until then, use `object_info` for validation and missing-input diagnostics, not for unfiltered positional indexing.
5. Add a diagnostic pass that compares compiled API inputs against schema inputs and reports unresolved `widget_N` inputs where a required named widget input is missing or where parity tooling would otherwise see false drift.
6. Add tests proving link-only object_info inputs do not shift widget positions.
7. Move existing Wan/WanAnimate positional aliases out of private workflow-only state while preserving current behavior.

## Phase 4: CLI, Discoverability, and Live Validation Loop

### Step 10: Add explicit CLI entry points (`vibecomfy/commands/port.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Register `port` as an explicit command in `COMMANDS`.
2. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port check <workflow> [--json] [--head-check-models]` for no-write preflight.
3. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port convert <workflow> --out out/scratchpads/name.py [--json] [--head-check-models]` for scratchpad Python output.
4. Add `python -m vibecomfy.cli port convert <workflow> --out ready_templates/kind/name.py --ready-id kind/name [--json]` for ready-template candidate output.
5. Make command help explain when to use `port check`, `port convert`, `doctor`, `validate`, `nodes install-plan`, `fetch`, and `--head-check-models`.
6. Return nonzero for hard errors: unresolved runtime class types, unresolved helper broadcasts, schema-required missing inputs, failed Python build, failed emitted scratchpad/template validation, or failed model HEAD checks when the user requested them.

### Step 11: Wire the live validation loop without making network checks mandatory (`vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py`, `vibecomfy/commands/run.py`, `scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep existing `workflow.validate(schema_provider=...)` as the hard gate before queueing runtime prompts.
2. Add `doctor` guidance that points users to `port check` when failures involve unknown classes, missing required inputs, helper-node issues, or model assets.
3. Add a cheap port-report summary to RunPod corpus matrix logs and copy the emitted scratchpad/report next to existing convert/validate logs.
4. Keep model URL HEAD checks opt-in and out of normal `run`, `doctor`, and `fetch` behavior.

### Step 12: Make discoverability first-class and sync every agent guidance surface (`README.md`, `docs/authoring.md`, `docs/adding_templates_models.md`, `docs/errors_and_doctor.md`, `docs/template_porting_workbench.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md`, `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py`, `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a new docs page for the porting workbench with a direct import -> `port check` -> `port convert` -> `validate` / `doctor` -> optional RunPod loop.
2. Update authoring and template docs so humans and agents start with `port check` before manual template editing or RunPod validation.
3. Update failure/live-validation docs to map common failures to commands: schema issues to `port check`/`validate`, custom nodes to `nodes install-plan`, model URLs to `port check --head-check-models`, Python materialization to `port convert`, and downloads to `fetch` only when URL-bearing assets are available.
4. Update the source agent guidance that feeds AGENTS/OpenAI/Claude-facing copies, then run `python scripts/sync_agent_skill.py --apply` so `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` is refreshed.
5. Extend `scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` default check mode or `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py` so the README-directed `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` local copy is checked for freshness. Do not document or rely on a nonexistent `--check` flag unless the implementation explicitly adds and tests that alias.
6. Ensure command help text mirrors the docs so discoverability is present in both CLI and repository guidance.

## Phase 5: Tests and Battle Targets

### Step 13: Add focused unit and CLI tests (`tests/test_porting_workbench.py`, `tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_workflow_core.py`, `tests/test_schema.py`, `tests/test_model_assets.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Test a simple fixture based on `image/z_image` or a tiny synthetic workflow: report shape, provenance, scratchpad Python emission, import/build, compile, and validate.
2. Test `video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_wan_animate_preprocess_kijai`: helper nodes are diagnosed separately, helper nodes do not survive compiled API, no compiled API link points to a removed helper node, required real node packs are suggested, and detection model assets are reported.
3. Test custom-node inference ignores known helper/UI nodes but still fails unresolved runtime classes.
4. Test porting-only API-shaped workflow model asset candidates, including filename-only assets with missing URL warnings, without changing `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` return shape.
5. Test widget alias diagnostics with a fake schema provider and keep existing Wan/WanAnimate alias expectations passing.
6. Test that raw object_info order containing link-only inputs does not drive positional widget mapping or shift widget aliases.
7. Test HEAD checks with injected/mocked HTTP behavior, not real network.
8. Add CLI smoke tests for `port check --json`, scratchpad-mode `port convert --out ... --json`, ready-template-mode `port convert --ready-id ...`, and command help discoverability.

### Step 14: Update parity, conversion, and sync tests (`tests/test_ready_templates.py`, `tools/_compile_equivalence.py`, `tools/convert_ready_templates.py`, `tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep ready-template parity tests passing after widget schema migration.
2. Verify `tools/convert_ready_templates.py --template ... --dry-run` still uses the moved ready-template emitter and reports equivalent diffs.
3. Add regression coverage that `vibecomfy convert` keeps its existing lightweight contract while `vibecomfy port convert` emits explicit scratchpad Python by default.
4. Add regression coverage that ready-template emission is only used by `port convert --ready-id` and ready-template tooling.
5. Add freshness coverage for `.claude/skills/vibecomfy/SKILL.md` or the sync path that regenerates it.

## Execution Order
1. Add report and helper diagnostics first, because helper filtering changes the correctness of every downstream diagnostic.
2. Add porting-only model asset analysis while preserving legacy `extract_from_raw_workflow(...)` behavior for `doctor` and `fetch`.
3. Split emitter code into explicit scratchpad and ready-template modes before wiring `port convert`.
4. Move widget schema code with compatibility wrappers and parity tests in the same change set, keeping the schema widget-only and not based on unfiltered object_info order.
5. Add the workbench orchestrator and `port check` before adding `port convert` writes.
6. Add opt-in HEAD checks after offline report behavior is covered.
7. Wire `doctor` / RunPod summaries after the report shape is stable.
8. Finish with discoverability docs, synced agent guidance, and battle-target tests.

## Validation Order
1. Run focused porting tests first: `pytest -q tests/test_porting_workbench.py tests/test_model_assets.py tests/test_workflow_core.py tests/test_schema.py`.
2. Run CLI tests: `pytest -q tests/test_cli.py`.
3. Run ready-template and parity regressions: `pytest -q tests/test_ready_templates.py` plus `python -m tools.convert_ready_templates --template image/qwen_image_2512 --dry-run`.
4. Run agent guidance sync tests: `python scripts/sync_agent_skill.py` and `pytest -q tests/test_agent_skill_sync.py`.
5. Run node/model checks: `pytest -q tests/test_nodes_install.py tests/test_models_registry.py`.
6. Run the full local suite with `pytest`.
7. Manual/optional network check: `python -m vibecomfy.cli port check video/wanvideo_wrapper_22_wan_animate_preprocess_kijai --json --head-check-models`.

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```
